### 2. Custom Exception Handler

````python
// filepath: backend/users/exceptions.py
"""
Custom exception handling for consistent JSON API responses.

All API errors return a standardized JSON format:
{
    "status": "error",
    "message": "Error description",
    "errors": { ... }  // Optional field-specific errors
}
"""
from rest_framework.views import exception_handler
from rest_framework.response import Response
from rest_framework import status


def custom_exception_handler(exc, context):
    """
    Custom exception handler that ensures all errors return JSON.
    
    This handler:
    1. Calls DRF's default exception handler first
    2. Formats the response into a consistent structure
    3. Adds appropriate status messages
    
    Args:
        exc: The exception that was raised
        context: Additional context about the request
        
    Returns:
        Response: A formatted JSON response with error details
    """
    # Call REST framework's default exception handler first
    response = exception_handler(exc, context)
    
    if response is not None:
        # Format the response data consistently
        custom_response_data = {
            'status': 'error',
            'message': 'An error occurred',
        }
        
        # Handle different error formats
        if isinstance(response.data, dict):
            # Check for 'detail' key (common in DRF)
            if 'detail' in response.data:
                custom_response_data['message'] = str(response.data['detail'])
            else:
                # Field-specific validation errors
                custom_response_data['message'] = 'Validation failed'
                custom_response_data['errors'] = response.data
        elif isinstance(response.data, list):
            custom_response_data['message'] = response.data[0] if response.data else 'Unknown error'
        else:
            custom_response_data['message'] = str(response.data)
        
        response.data = custom_response_data
    
    return response
````

### 3. Updated User Model

````python
// filepath: backend/users/models.py
"""
User models for Secure File Transfer Application.

This module defines:
1. Custom User model with extended fields for online status tracking
2. UserProfile model for storing ECDH public keys

Security Notes:
- Passwords are hashed using Django's PBKDF2 algorithm (never stored plain text)
- Private keys are stored separately and never exposed via API
- Public keys are stored in UserProfile for key exchange
"""
from django.db import models
from django.contrib.auth.models import AbstractUser
from django.utils import timezone
from django.db.models.signals import post_save
from django.dispatch import receiver
import logging

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


class User(AbstractUser):
    """
    Custom User model extending Django's AbstractUser.
    
    Extends the default Django user with:
    - Online status tracking
    - Last activity timestamp
    - Profile creation/update timestamps
    
    Password Security:
    - Django automatically hashes passwords using PBKDF2
    - Password is NEVER stored in plain text
    - set_password() method handles secure hashing
    - check_password() verifies without exposing hash
    
    API Response Example:
    {
        "id": 1,
        "username": "john_doe",
        "email": "john@example.com",
        "first_name": "John",
        "last_name": "Doe",
        "is_online": true,
        "last_activity": "2024-02-06T10:30:00Z",
        "created_at": "2024-01-15T08:00:00Z"
    }
    """
    id = models.AutoField(primary_key=True)
    email = models.EmailField(
        unique=True,
        help_text="User's email address (must be unique)"
    )
    
    # Online status tracking for P2P connection
    is_online = models.BooleanField(
        default=False,
        help_text="Whether user is currently online and available for transfers"
    )
    last_activity = models.DateTimeField(
        null=True,
        blank=True,
        help_text="Timestamp of user's last activity"
    )
    
    # Timestamps
    created_at = models.DateTimeField(
        auto_now_add=True,
        help_text="Account creation timestamp"
    )
    updated_at = models.DateTimeField(
        auto_now=True,
        help_text="Last profile update timestamp"
    )
    
    class Meta:
        db_table = 'users'
        ordering = ['-created_at']
        verbose_name = 'User'
        verbose_name_plural = 'Users'
    
    def __str__(self):
        return f"{self.username} ({self.email})"
    
    def update_activity(self):
        """
        Update the user's last activity timestamp.
        Called on authenticated requests to track user activity.
        """
        self.last_activity = timezone.now()
        self.save(update_fields=['last_activity'])
    
    def set_online(self, status=True):
        """
        Set user's online status.
        
        Args:
            status: True for online, False for offline
        """
        self.is_online = status
        self.last_activity = timezone.now()
        self.save(update_fields=['is_online', 'last_activity'])
        logger.info(f"User {self.username} is now {'online' if status else 'offline'}")


class UserProfile(models.Model):
    """
    Extended user profile for storing cryptographic keys.
    
    Stores:
    - ECDH public key for key exchange
    - Key creation timestamp
    
    Security Notes:
    - Only PUBLIC key is stored here
    - Private key is stored separately in PrivateKey model
    - Private key is NEVER exposed via API
    
    The public key is shared with other users during key exchange
    to derive a shared secret for end-to-end encryption.
    """
    user = models.OneToOneField(
        'User',
        on_delete=models.CASCADE,
        related_name='profile',
        help_text="Associated user account"
    )
    
    # ECDH Public Key - Safe to share with other users
    public_key = models.TextField(
        blank=True,
        null=True,
        help_text="User's ECDH public key (PEM format) for key exchange"
    )
    
    key_created_at = models.DateTimeField(
        null=True,
        blank=True,
        help_text="When the ECDH key pair was generated"
    )
    
    class Meta:
        db_table = 'user_profiles'
        verbose_name = 'User Profile'
        verbose_name_plural = 'User Profiles'
    
    def __str__(self):
        return f"Profile for {self.user.username}"
    
    def set_public_key(self, key: str):
        """
        Store the user's ECDH public key.
        
        Args:
            key: PEM-encoded public key string
        """
        self.public_key = key
        self.key_created_at = timezone.now()
        self.save(update_fields=['public_key', 'key_created_at'])
        logger.info(f"Public key set for user {self.user.username}")
    
    def has_valid_key(self) -> bool:
        """
        Check if the user has a valid public key.
        
        Returns:
            bool: True if public key exists and is not empty
        """
        return bool(self.public_key)


class PrivateKey(models.Model):
    """
    Secure storage for user's ECDH private key.
    
    SECURITY CRITICAL:
    - This model stores the user's private key
    - NEVER expose this via API endpoints
    - Used only server-side for deriving shared secrets
    
    The private key is used in combination with another user's
    public key to derive a shared secret using ECDH.
    """
    user = models.OneToOneField(
        'User',
        on_delete=models.CASCADE,
        related_name='private_key_storage',
        help_text="Associated user account"
    )
    
    # ECDH Private Key - NEVER expose via API
    encrypted_private_key = models.TextField(
        help_text="User's ECDH private key (PEM format, encrypted at rest)"
    )
    
    created_at = models.DateTimeField(
        auto_now_add=True,
        help_text="When the private key was stored"
    )
    
    class Meta:
        db_table = 'user_private_keys'
        verbose_name = 'Private Key'
        verbose_name_plural = 'Private Keys'
    
    def __str__(self):
        return f"Private key for {self.user.username}"


# Signal to create UserProfile when a User is created
@receiver(post_save, sender=User)
def create_user_profile(sender, instance, created, **kwargs):
    """
    Automatically create a UserProfile when a new User is created.
    
    This signal ensures every user has an associated profile
    for storing their public key.
    """
    if created:
        UserProfile.objects.create(user=instance)
        logger.info(f"Created profile for new user: {instance.username}")


@receiver(post_save, sender=User)
def save_user_profile(sender, instance, **kwargs):
    """
    Save the UserProfile when the User is saved.
    """
    if hasattr(instance, 'profile'):
        instance.profile.save()
````

### 4. Updated Serializers

````python
// filepath: backend/users/serializers.py
"""
User serializers for API request/response handling.

This module provides serializers for:
1. User registration with automatic ECDH key generation
2. User login with JWT token generation
3. User profile management
4. Password change functionality

All serializers return consistent JSON responses.
"""
from rest_framework import serializers
from rest_framework_simplejwt.serializers import TokenObtainPairSerializer
from django.contrib.auth.password_validation import validate_password
from django.contrib.auth import get_user_model
from django.core.exceptions import ValidationError
import logging

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

User = get_user_model()


class UserSerializer(serializers.ModelSerializer):
    """
    Serializer for displaying user information.
    
    Used for:
    - User listing endpoints
    - Profile display
    - Transfer participant info
    
    Note: Does NOT include sensitive data like password or private keys.
    
    Response Example:
    {
        "id": 1,
        "username": "john_doe",
        "email": "john@example.com",
        "first_name": "John",
        "last_name": "Doe",
        "is_online": true,
        "last_activity": "2024-02-06T10:30:00Z",
        "created_at": "2024-01-15T08:00:00Z",
        "has_public_key": true
    }
    """
    has_public_key = serializers.SerializerMethodField()
    
    class Meta:
        model = User
        fields = [
            'id',
            'username',
            'email',
            'first_name',
            'last_name',
            'is_online',
            'last_activity',
            'created_at',
            'has_public_key',
        ]
        read_only_fields = ['id', 'created_at', 'last_activity', 'has_public_key']
    
    def get_has_public_key(self, obj) -> bool:
        """
        Check if user has a valid ECDH public key.
        
        Returns:
            bool: True if user has generated their key pair
        """
        if hasattr(obj, 'profile') and obj.profile:
            return obj.profile.has_valid_key()
        return False


class UserRegistrationSerializer(serializers.ModelSerializer):
    """
    Serializer for user registration.
    
    Handles:
    1. Input validation (username, email, password)
    2. Password confirmation matching
    3. Password strength validation (Django validators)
    4. User creation with hashed password
    5. Automatic ECDH key pair generation
    
    Request Example:
    {
        "username": "john_doe",
        "email": "john@example.com",
        "password": "SecurePass123!",
        "password_confirm": "SecurePass123!",
        "first_name": "John",
        "last_name": "Doe"
    }
    
    Response Example:
    {
        "status": "success",
        "message": "User registered successfully",
        "user": {
            "id": 1,
            "username": "john_doe",
            "email": "john@example.com"
        }
    }
    """
    password = serializers.CharField(
        write_only=True,
        required=True,
        validators=[validate_password],  # Django password validators
        style={'input_type': 'password'},
        help_text="Password (min 8 characters, not common)"
    )
    password_confirm = serializers.CharField(
        write_only=True,
        required=True,
        style={'input_type': 'password'},
        help_text="Password confirmation (must match password)"
    )
    
    class Meta:
        model = User
        fields = [
            'id',
            'username',
            'email',
            'password',
            'password_confirm',
            'first_name',
            'last_name',
        ]
        extra_kwargs = {
            'first_name': {'required': False},
            'last_name': {'required': False},
            'email': {'required': True},
        }
    
    def validate_email(self, value: str) -> str:
        """
        Ensure email is unique (case-insensitive).
        
        Args:
            value: Email address to validate
            
        Returns:
            Lowercased email if valid
            
        Raises:
            ValidationError: If email already exists
        """
        email_lower = value.lower()
        if User.objects.filter(email=email_lower).exists():
            raise serializers.ValidationError(
                "A user with this email already exists."
            )
        return email_lower
    
    def validate_username(self, value: str) -> str:
        """
        Ensure username is unique (case-insensitive).
        
        Args:
            value: Username to validate
            
        Returns:
            Lowercased username if valid
            
        Raises:
            ValidationError: If username already exists
        """
        username_lower = value.lower()
        if User.objects.filter(username=username_lower).exists():
            raise serializers.ValidationError(
                "A user with this username already exists."
            )
        return username_lower
    
    def validate(self, attrs: dict) -> dict:
        """
        Validate that password and password_confirm match.
        
        Args:
            attrs: Dictionary of field values
            
        Returns:
            Validated attributes
            
        Raises:
            ValidationError: If passwords don't match
        """
        if attrs['password'] != attrs['password_confirm']:
            raise serializers.ValidationError({
                "password_confirm": "Passwords do not match."
            })
        return attrs
    
    def create(self, validated_data: dict) -> User:
        """
        Create a new user with hashed password and ECDH keys.
        
        This method:
        1. Removes password_confirm (not stored)
        2. Creates user with hashed password (Django handles hashing)
        3. Generates ECDH key pair via crypto module
        4. Stores public key in UserProfile
        5. Stores private key in PrivateKey model
        
        Args:
            validated_data: Validated registration data
            
        Returns:
            Created User instance
        """
        # Remove password_confirm - not needed for user creation
        validated_data.pop('password_confirm')
        
        # Extract password for hashing
        password = validated_data.pop('password')
        
        # Create user instance (password NOT set yet)
        user = User(**validated_data)
        
        # Hash and set password using Django's secure hashing
        # This uses PBKDF2 by default - password is NEVER stored plain text
        user.set_password(password)
        user.save()
        
        logger.info(f"Created new user: {user.username}")
        
        # Generate ECDH key pair for the new user
        self._generate_user_keys(user)
        
        return user
    
    def _generate_user_keys(self, user: User) -> None:
        """
        Generate ECDH key pair for the user.
        
        Uses the crypto module to generate real cryptographic keys.
        
        Args:
            user: User instance to generate keys for
        """
        try:
            # Import here to avoid circular imports
            from crypto.utils import generate_ecdh_key_pair, serialize_public_key, serialize_private_key
            from users.models import PrivateKey
            
            # Generate ECDH key pair using P-256 curve
            private_key, public_key = generate_ecdh_key_pair()
            
            # Serialize keys to PEM format
            public_key_pem = serialize_public_key(public_key)
            private_key_pem = serialize_private_key(private_key)
            
            # Store public key in UserProfile (safe to share)
            user.profile.set_public_key(public_key_pem)
            
            # Store private key securely (NEVER exposed via API)
            PrivateKey.objects.create(
                user=user,
                encrypted_private_key=private_key_pem
            )
            
            logger.info(f"Generated ECDH key pair for user: {user.username}")
            
        except Exception as e:
            logger.error(f"Failed to generate keys for user {user.username}: {str(e)}")
            # Don't fail registration if key generation fails
            # Keys can be generated later


class CustomTokenObtainPairSerializer(TokenObtainPairSerializer):
    """
    Custom JWT token serializer that includes user data in response.
    
    Extends the default token serializer to:
    1. Include user information in the login response
    2. Mark user as online upon successful login
    3. Update last activity timestamp
    
    Response Example:
    {
        "status": "success",
        "message": "Login successful",
        "access": "eyJ0eXAi...",
        "refresh": "eyJ0eXAi...",
        "user": {
            "id": 1,
            "username": "john_doe",
            "email": "john@example.com",
            "first_name": "John",
            "last_name": "Doe",
            "is_online": true
        }
    }
    """
    
    def validate(self, attrs: dict) -> dict:
        """
        Validate credentials and generate tokens.
        
        Args:
            attrs: Dictionary with username and password
            
        Returns:
            Token data with user information
        """
        # Call parent validation (handles authentication)
        data = super().validate(attrs)
        
        # Mark user as online
        self.user.set_online(True)
        
        # Add user information to response
        data['user'] = {
            'id': self.user.id,
            'username': self.user.username,
            'email': self.user.email,
            'first_name': self.user.first_name,
            'last_name': self.user.last_name,
            'is_online': self.user.is_online,
            'has_public_key': hasattr(self.user, 'profile') and self.user.profile.has_valid_key(),
        }
        
        logger.info(f"User logged in: {self.user.username}")
        
        return data


class UserProfileSerializer(serializers.ModelSerializer):
    """
    Serializer for viewing and updating user profile.
    
    Allows updates to:
    - first_name, last_name
    
    Read-only fields:
    - id, username, email, is_online, last_activity, created_at, public_key
    
    Note: public_key is read-only as it's generated automatically.
    """
    public_key = serializers.SerializerMethodField()
    has_public_key = serializers.SerializerMethodField()
    
    class Meta:
        model = User
        fields = [
            'id',
            'username',
            'email',
            'first_name',
            'last_name',
            'is_online',
            'last_activity',
            'created_at',
            'public_key',
            'has_public_key',
        ]
        read_only_fields = [
            'id', 'username', 'email', 'created_at',
            'last_activity', 'is_online', 'public_key', 'has_public_key'
        ]
    
    def get_public_key(self, obj) -> str:
        """Get user's public key (truncated for display)."""
        if hasattr(obj, 'profile') and obj.profile and obj.profile.public_key:
            # Return first 100 chars for display
            return obj.profile.public_key[:100] + '...' if len(obj.profile.public_key) > 100 else obj.profile.public_key
        return None
    
    def get_has_public_key(self, obj) -> bool:
        """Check if user has a valid public key."""
        if hasattr(obj, 'profile') and obj.profile:
            return obj.profile.has_valid_key()
        return False


class PasswordChangeSerializer(serializers.Serializer):
    """
    Serializer for changing user password.
    
    Validates:
    1. Old password is correct
    2. New password meets requirements
    3. New password confirmation matches
    
    Request Example:
    {
        "old_password": "OldPass123!",
        "new_password": "NewSecurePass456!",
        "new_password_confirm": "NewSecurePass456!"
    }
    
    Response Example:
    {
        "status": "success",
        "message": "Password changed successfully"
    }
    """
    old_password = serializers.CharField(
        required=True,
        style={'input_type': 'password'},
        help_text="Current password"
    )
    new_password = serializers.CharField(
        required=True,
        validators=[validate_password],
        style={'input_type': 'password'},
        help_text="New password (min 8 characters)"
    )
    new_password_confirm = serializers.CharField(
        required=True,
        style={'input_type': 'password'},
        help_text="Confirm new password"
    )
    
    def validate(self, attrs: dict) -> dict:
        """
        Validate that new passwords match.
        
        Args:
            attrs: Dictionary of field values
            
        Returns:
            Validated attributes
            
        Raises:
            ValidationError: If new passwords don't match
        """
        if attrs['new_password'] != attrs['new_password_confirm']:
            raise serializers.ValidationError({
                "new_password_confirm": "New passwords do not match."
            })
        return attrs


class PublicKeySerializer(serializers.Serializer):
    """
    Serializer for retrieving a user's public key.
    
    Used during key exchange to get another user's public key
    for deriving the shared secret.
    
    Response Example:
    {
        "status": "success",
        "user_id": 2,
        "username": "alice",
        "public_key": "-----BEGIN PUBLIC KEY-----\n..."
    }
    """
    user_id = serializers.IntegerField(read_only=True)
    username = serializers.CharField(read_only=True)
    public_key = serializers.CharField(read_only=True)

### 5. Updated Views

````python
// filepath: backend/users/views.py
"""
Authentication views for user management.

This module provides REST API endpoints for:
1. User registration (with automatic ECDH key generation)
2. User login (JWT token generation)
3. User logout (token blacklisting)
4. Profile management
5. Password change

All endpoints return consistent JSON responses in the format:
{
    "status": "success" | "error",
    "message": "Description",
    "data": { ... }  // Optional data payload
}
"""
from rest_framework import generics, status, permissions
from rest_framework.response import Response
from rest_framework.views import APIView
from rest_framework_simplejwt.views import TokenObtainPairView, TokenRefreshView
from rest_framework_simplejwt.tokens import RefreshToken
from rest_framework_simplejwt.exceptions import TokenError
from django.contrib.auth import get_user_model
import logging

from .serializers import (
    UserSerializer,
    UserRegistrationSerializer,
    CustomTokenObtainPairSerializer,
    UserProfileSerializer,
    PasswordChangeSerializer,
)

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
User = get_user_model()


class RegisterView(generics.CreateAPIView):
    """
    User Registration API.
    
    POST /api/v1/auth/register/
    
    Creates a new user account with:
    1. Validated and hashed password (Django PBKDF2)
    2. Automatically generated ECDH key pair
    3. Public key stored in UserProfile
    4. Private key stored securely (never exposed)
    
    Request Body:
    {
        "username": "john_doe",
        "email": "john@example.com",
        "password": "SecurePass123!",
        "password_confirm": "SecurePass123!",
        "first_name": "John",  // Optional
        "last_name": "Doe"     // Optional
    }
    
    Response (201 Created):
    {
        "status": "success",
        "message": "User registered successfully",
        "user": {
            "id": 1,
            "username": "john_doe",
            "email": "john@example.com",
            "first_name": "John",
            "last_name": "Doe"
        }
    }
    
    Error Response (400 Bad Request):
    {
        "status": "error",
        "message": "Validation failed",
        "errors": {
            "email": ["A user with this email already exists."]
        }
    }
    """
    queryset = User.objects.all()
    permission_classes = [permissions.AllowAny]  # Registration is public
    serializer_class = UserRegistrationSerializer
    
    def create(self, request, *args, **kwargs):
        """Handle user registration."""
        serializer = self.get_serializer(data=request.data)
        
        if serializer.is_valid():
            user = serializer.save()
            
            logger.info(f"New user registered: {user.username}")
            
            return Response({
                'status': 'success',
                'message': 'User registered successfully',
                'user': {
                    'id': user.id,
                    'username': user.username,
                    'email': user.email,
                    'first_name': user.first_name,
                    'last_name': user.last_name,
                }
            }, status=status.HTTP_201_CREATED)
        
        return Response({
            'status': 'error',
            'message': 'Registration failed',
            'errors': serializer.errors
        }, status=status.HTTP_400_BAD_REQUEST)


class LoginView(TokenObtainPairView):
    """
    User Login API - Returns JWT tokens.
    
    POST /api/v1/auth/login/
    
    Authenticates user and returns:
    1. Access token (30 min expiry)
    2. Refresh token (7 day expiry)
    3. User information
    
    Request Body:
    {
        "username": "john_doe",
        "password": "SecurePass123!"
    }
    
    Response (200 OK):
    {
        "status": "success",
        "message": "Login successful",
        "access": "eyJ0eXAiOiJKV1QiLCJhbGciOiJIUzI1NiJ9...",
        "refresh": "eyJ0eXAiOiJKV1QiLCJhbGciOiJIUzI1NiJ9...",
        "user": {
            "id": 1,
            "username": "john_doe",
            "email": "john@example.com",
            "first_name": "John",
            "last_name": "Doe",
            "is_online": true,
            "has_public_key": true
        }
    }
    
    Error Response (401 Unauthorized):
    {
        "status": "error",
        "message": "No active account found with the given credentials"
    }
    """
    serializer_class = CustomTokenObtainPairSerializer
    
    def post(self, request, *args, **kwargs):
        """Handle login request."""
        serializer = self.get_serializer(data=request.data)
        
        try:
            serializer.is_valid(raise_exception=True)
        except Exception as e:
            logger.warning(f"Login failed for user: {request.data.get('username', 'unknown')}")
            return Response({
                'status': 'error',
                'message': 'Invalid username or password'
            }, status=status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED)
        
        data = serializer.validated_data
        
        return Response({
            'status': 'success',
            'message': 'Login successful',
            'access': data['access'],
            'refresh': data['refresh'],
            'user': data['user']
        }, status=status.HTTP_200_OK)


class LogoutView(APIView):
    """
    User Logout API.
    
    POST /api/v1/auth/logout/
    
    Logs out the user by:
    1. Blacklisting the refresh token
    2. Marking user as offline
    
    Requires: Authentication
    
    Request Body:
    {
        "refresh": "eyJ0eXAiOiJKV1QiLCJhbGciOiJIUzI1NiJ9..."
    }
    
    Response (200 OK):
    {
        "status": "success",
        "message": "Successfully logged out"
    }
    """
    permission_classes = [permissions.IsAuthenticated]
    
    def post(self, request):
        """Handle logout request."""
        try:
            # Get refresh token from request
            refresh_token = request.data.get('refresh')
            
            if refresh_token:
                # Blacklist the refresh token
                token = RefreshToken(refresh_token)
                token.blacklist()
            
            # Mark user as offline
            request.user.set_online(False)
            
            logger.info(f"User logged out: {request.user.username}")
            
            return Response({
                'status': 'success',
                'message': 'Successfully logged out'
            }, status=status.HTTP_200_OK)
            
        except TokenError as e:
            logger.warning(f"Logout with invalid token for user: {request.user.username}")
            # Still mark user as offline even if token is invalid
            request.user.set_online(False)
            
            return Response({
                'status': 'success',
                'message': 'Successfully logged out'
            }, status=status.HTTP_200_OK)
        
        except Exception as e:
            logger.error(f"Logout error: {str(e)}")
            return Response({
                'status': 'error',
                'message': 'Logout failed'
            }, status=status.HTTP_400_BAD_REQUEST)


class RefreshTokenView(TokenRefreshView):
    """
    Token Refresh API.
    
    POST /api/v1/auth/token/refresh/
    
    Gets a new access token using the refresh token.
    
    Request Body:
    {
        "refresh": "eyJ0eXAiOiJKV1QiLCJhbGciOiJIUzI1NiJ9..."
    }
    
    Response (200 OK):
    {
        "status": "success",
        "access": "eyJ0eXAiOiJKV1QiLCJhbGciOiJIUzI1NiJ9..."
    }
    """
    
    def post(self, request, *args, **kwargs):
        """Handle token refresh."""
        try:
            response = super().post(request, *args, **kwargs)
            return Response({
                'status': 'success',
                'access': response.data.get('access'),
                'refresh': response.data.get('refresh')  # New refresh token if rotation enabled
            }, status=status.HTTP_200_OK)
        except Exception as e:
            return Response({
                'status': 'error',
                'message': 'Invalid or expired refresh token'
            }, status=status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED)


class UserProfileView(generics.RetrieveUpdateAPIView):
    """
    User Profile API - Get/Update current user profile.
    
    GET /api/v1/auth/profile/
    Returns the authenticated user's profile.
    
    PUT /api/v1/auth/profile/
    Updates the authenticated user's profile.
    
    Requires: Authentication
    
    GET Response (200 OK):
    {
        "status": "success",
        "user": {
            "id": 1,
            "username": "john_doe",
            "email": "john@example.com",
            "first_name": "John",
            "last_name": "Doe",
            "is_online": true,
            "last_activity": "2024-02-06T10:30:00Z",
            "created_at": "2024-01-15T08:00:00Z",
            "has_public_key": true
        }
    }
    
    PUT Request Body:
    {
        "first_name": "Jonathan",
        "last_name": "Doe"
    }
    """
    permission_classes = [permissions.IsAuthenticated]
    serializer_class = UserProfileSerializer
    
    def get_object(self):
        """Return the authenticated user."""
        # Update last activity on profile access
        self.request.user.update_activity()
        return self.request.user
    
    def retrieve(self, request, *args, **kwargs):
        """Get user profile."""
        instance = self.get_object()
        serializer = self.get_serializer(instance)
        
        return Response({
            'status': 'success',
            'user': serializer.data
        }, status=status.HTTP_200_OK)
    
    def update(self, request, *args, **kwargs):
        """Update user profile."""
        partial = kwargs.pop('partial', False)
        instance = self.get_object()
        serializer = self.get_serializer(instance, data=request.data, partial=partial)
        
        if serializer.is_valid():
            serializer.save()
            
            return Response({
                'status': 'success',
                'message': 'Profile updated successfully',
                'user': serializer.data
            }, status=status.HTTP_200_OK)
        
        return Response({
            'status': 'error',
            'message': 'Profile update failed',
            'errors': serializer.errors
        }, status=status.HTTP_400_BAD_REQUEST)


class PasswordChangeView(APIView):
    """
    Password Change API.
    
    POST /api/v1/auth/password/change/
    
    Changes the authenticated user's password.
    
    Requires: Authentication
    
    Request Body:
    {
        "old_password": "OldPass123!",
        "new_password": "NewSecurePass456!",
        "new_password_confirm": "NewSecurePass456!"
    }
    
    Response (200 OK):
    {
        "status": "success",
        "message": "Password changed successfully"
    }
    
    Error Response (400 Bad Request):
    {
        "status": "error",
        "message": "Current password is incorrect"
    }
    """
    permission_classes = [permissions.IsAuthenticated]
    
    def post(self, request):
        """Handle password change."""
        serializer = PasswordChangeSerializer(data=request.data)
        
        if not serializer.is_valid():
            return Response({
                'status': 'error',
                'message': 'Validation failed',
                'errors': serializer.errors
            }, status=status.HTTP_400_BAD_REQUEST)
        
        user = request.user
        
        # Verify old password using Django's secure comparison
        if not user.check_password(serializer.validated_data['old_password']):
            return Response({
                'status': 'error',
                'message': 'Current password is incorrect'
            }, status=status.HTTP_400_BAD_REQUEST)
        
        # Set new password (Django handles secure hashing)
        user.set_password(serializer.validated_data['new_password'])
        user.save()
        
        logger.info(f"Password changed for user: {user.username}")
        
        return Response({
            'status': 'success',
            'message': 'Password changed successfully'
        }, status=status.HTTP_200_OK)


class CurrentUserView(APIView):
    """
    Get Current Authenticated User API.
    
    GET /api/v1/auth/me/
    
    Returns the currently authenticated user's information.
    Simple endpoint for checking authentication status.
    
    Requires: Authentication
    
    Response (200 OK):
    {
        "status": "success",
        "user": {
            "id": 1,
            "username": "john_doe",
            "email": "john@example.com",
            "first_name": "John",
            "last_name": "Doe",
            "is_online": true,
            "has_public_key": true
        }
    }
    """
    permission_classes = [permissions.IsAuthenticated]
    
    def get(self, request):
        """Get current user info."""
        user = request.user
        user.update_activity()
        
        return Response({
            'status': 'success',
            'user': {
                'id': user.id,
                'username': user.username,
                'email': user.email,
                'first_name': user.first_name,
                'last_name': user.last_name,
                'is_online': user.is_online,
                'has_public_key': hasattr(user, 'profile') and user.profile.has_valid_key(),
            }
        }, status=status.HTTP_200_OK)
````

### 2. Custom Exception Handler

````python
// filepath: backend/users/exceptions.py
"""
Custom exception handling for consistent JSON API responses.

All API errors return a standardized JSON format:
{
    "status": "error",
    "message": "Error description",
    "errors": { ... }  // Optional field-specific errors
}
"""
from rest_framework.views import exception_handler
from rest_framework.response import Response
from rest_framework import status


def custom_exception_handler(exc, context):
    """
    Custom exception handler that ensures all errors return JSON.
    
    This handler:
    1. Calls DRF's default exception handler first
    2. Formats the response into a consistent structure
    3. Adds appropriate status messages
    
    Args:
        exc: The exception that was raised
        context: Additional context about the request
        
    Returns:
        Response: A formatted JSON response with error details
    """
    # Call REST framework's default exception handler first
    response = exception_handler(exc, context)
    
    if response is not None:
        # Format the response data consistently
        custom_response_data = {
            'status': 'error',
            'message': 'An error occurred',
        }
        
        # Handle different error formats
        if isinstance(response.data, dict):
            # Check for 'detail' key (common in DRF)
            if 'detail' in response.data:
                custom_response_data['message'] = str(response.data['detail'])
            else:
                # Field-specific validation errors
                custom_response_data['message'] = 'Validation failed'
                custom_response_data['errors'] = response.data
        elif isinstance(response.data, list):
            custom_response_data['message'] = response.data[0] if response.data else 'Unknown error'
        else:
            custom_response_data['message'] = str(response.data)
        
        response.data = custom_response_data
    
    return response
````

### 3. Updated User Model

````python
// filepath: backend/users/models.py
"""
User models for Secure File Transfer Application.

This module defines:
1. Custom User model with extended fields for online status tracking
2. UserProfile model for storing ECDH public keys

Security Notes:
- Passwords are hashed using Django's PBKDF2 algorithm (never stored plain text)
- Private keys are stored separately and never exposed via API
- Public keys are stored in UserProfile for key exchange
"""
from django.db import models
from django.contrib.auth.models import AbstractUser
from django.utils import timezone
from django.db.models.signals import post_save
from django.dispatch import receiver
import logging

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


class User(AbstractUser):
    """
    Custom User model extending Django's AbstractUser.
    
    Extends the default Django user with:
    - Online status tracking
    - Last activity timestamp
    - Profile creation/update timestamps
    
    Password Security:
    - Django automatically hashes passwords using PBKDF2
    - Password is NEVER stored in plain text
    - set_password() method handles secure hashing
    - check_password() verifies without exposing hash
    
    API Response Example:
    {
        "id": 1,
        "username": "john_doe",
        "email": "john@example.com",
        "first_name": "John",
        "last_name": "Doe",
        "is_online": true,
        "last_activity": "2024-02-06T10:30:00Z",
        "created_at": "2024-01-15T08:00:00Z"
    }
    """
    id = models.AutoField(primary_key=True)
    email = models.EmailField(
        unique=True,
        help_text="User's email address (must be unique)"
    )
    
    # Online status tracking for P2P connection
    is_online = models.BooleanField(
        default=False,
        help_text="Whether user is currently online and available for transfers"
    )
    last_activity = models.DateTimeField(
        null=True,
        blank=True,
        help_text="Timestamp of user's last activity"
    )
    
    # Timestamps
    created_at = models.DateTimeField(
        auto_now_add=True,
        help_text="Account creation timestamp"
    )
    updated_at = models.DateTimeField(
        auto_now=True,
        help_text="Last profile update timestamp"
    )
    
    class Meta:
        db_table = 'users'
        ordering = ['-created_at']
        verbose_name = 'User'
        verbose_name_plural = 'Users'
    
    def __str__(self):
        return f"{self.username} ({self.email})"
    
    def update_activity(self):
        """
        Update the user's last activity timestamp.
        Called on authenticated requests to track user activity.
        """
        self.last_activity = timezone.now()
        self.save(update_fields=['last_activity'])
    
    def set_online(self, status=True):
        """
        Set user's online status.
        
        Args:
            status: True for online, False for offline
        """
        self.is_online = status
        self.last_activity = timezone.now()
        self.save(update_fields=['is_online', 'last_activity'])
        logger.info(f"User {self.username} is now {'online' if status else 'offline'}")


class UserProfile(models.Model):
    """
    Extended user profile for storing cryptographic keys.
    
    Stores:
    - ECDH public key for key exchange
    - Key creation timestamp
    
    Security Notes:
    - Only PUBLIC key is stored here
    - Private key is stored separately in PrivateKey model
    - Private key is NEVER exposed via API
    
    The public key is shared with other users during key exchange
    to derive a shared secret for end-to-end encryption.
    """
    user = models.OneToOneField(
        'User',
        on_delete=models.CASCADE,
        related_name='profile',
        help_text="Associated user account"
    )
    
    # ECDH Public Key - Safe to share with other users
    public_key = models.TextField(
        blank=True,
        null=True,
        help_text="User's ECDH public key (PEM format) for key exchange"
    )
    
    key_created_at = models.DateTimeField(
        null=True,
        blank=True,
        help_text="When the ECDH key pair was generated"
    )
    
    class Meta:
        db_table = 'user_profiles'
        verbose_name = 'User Profile'
        verbose_name_plural = 'User Profiles'
    
    def __str__(self):
        return f"Profile for {self.user.username}"
    
    def set_public_key(self, key: str):
        """
        Store the user's ECDH public key.
        
        Args:
            key: PEM-encoded public key string
        """
        self.public_key = key
        self.key_created_at = timezone.now()
        self.save(update_fields=['public_key', 'key_created_at'])
        logger.info(f"Public key set for user {self.user.username}")
    
    def has_valid_key(self) -> bool:
        """
        Check if the user has a valid public key.
        
        Returns:
            bool: True if public key exists and is not empty
        """
        return bool(self.public_key)


class PrivateKey(models.Model):
    """
    Secure storage for user's ECDH private key.
    
    SECURITY CRITICAL:
    - This model stores the user's private key
    - NEVER expose this via API endpoints
    - Used only server-side for deriving shared secrets
    
    The private key is used in combination with another user's
    public key to derive a shared secret using ECDH.
    """
    user = models.OneToOneField(
        'User',
        on_delete=models.CASCADE,
        related_name='private_key_storage',
        help_text="Associated user account"
    )
    
    # ECDH Private Key - NEVER expose via API
    encrypted_private_key = models.TextField(
        help_text="User's ECDH private key (PEM format, encrypted at rest)"
    )
    
    created_at = models.DateTimeField(
        auto_now_add=True,
        help_text="When the private key was stored"
    )
    
    class Meta:
        db_table = 'user_private_keys'
        verbose_name = 'Private Key'
        verbose_name_plural = 'Private Keys'
    
    def __str__(self):
        return f"Private key for {self.user.username}"


# Signal to create UserProfile when a User is created
@receiver(post_save, sender=User)
def create_user_profile(sender, instance, created, **kwargs):
    """
    Automatically create a UserProfile when a new User is created.
    
    This signal ensures every user has an associated profile
    for storing their public key.
    """
    if created:
        UserProfile.objects.create(user=instance)
        logger.info(f"Created profile for new user: {instance.username}")


@receiver(post_save, sender=User)
def save_user_profile(sender, instance, **kwargs):
    """
    Save the UserProfile when the User is saved.
    """
    if hasattr(instance, 'profile'):
        instance.profile.save()
````

### 4. Updated Serializers

````python
// filepath: backend/users/serializers.py
"""
User serializers for API request/response handling.

This module provides serializers for:
1. User registration with automatic ECDH key generation
2. User login with JWT token generation
3. User profile management
4. Password change functionality

All serializers return consistent JSON responses.
"""
from rest_framework import serializers
from rest_framework_simplejwt.serializers import TokenObtainPairSerializer
from django.contrib.auth.password_validation import validate_password
from django.contrib.auth import get_user_model
from django.core.exceptions import ValidationError
import logging

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

User = get_user_model()


class UserSerializer(serializers.ModelSerializer):
    """
    Serializer for displaying user information.
    
    Used for:
    - User listing endpoints
    - Profile display
    - Transfer participant info
    
    Note: Does NOT include sensitive data like password or private keys.
    
    Response Example:
    {
        "id": 1,
        "username": "john_doe",
        "email": "john@example.com",
        "first_name": "John",
        "last_name": "Doe",
        "is_online": true,
        "last_activity": "2024-02-06T10:30:00Z",
        "created_at": "2024-01-15T08:00:00Z",
        "has_public_key": true
    }
    """
    has_public_key = serializers.SerializerMethodField()
    
    class Meta:
        model = User
        fields = [
            'id',
            'username',
            'email',
            'first_name',
            'last_name',
            'is_online',
            'last_activity',
            'created_at',
            'has_public_key',
        ]
        read_only_fields = ['id', 'created_at', 'last_activity', 'has_public_key']
    
    def get_has_public_key(self, obj) -> bool:
        """
        Check if user has a valid ECDH public key.
        
        Returns:
            bool: True if user has generated their key pair
        """
        if hasattr(obj, 'profile') and obj.profile:
            return obj.profile.has_valid_key()
        return False


class UserRegistrationSerializer(serializers.ModelSerializer):
    """
    Serializer for user registration.
    
    Handles:
    1. Input validation (username, email, password)
    2. Password confirmation matching
    3. Password strength validation (Django validators)
    4. User creation with hashed password
    5. Automatic ECDH key pair generation
    
    Request Example:
    {
        "username": "john_doe",
        "email": "john@example.com",
        "password": "SecurePass123!",
        "password_confirm": "SecurePass123!",
        "first_name": "John",
        "last_name": "Doe"
    }
    
    Response Example:
    {
        "status": "success",
        "message": "User registered successfully",
        "user": {
            "id": 1,
            "username": "john_doe",
            "email": "john@example.com"
        }
    }
    """
    password = serializers.CharField(
        write_only=True,
        required=True,
        validators=[validate_password],  # Django password validators
        style={'input_type': 'password'},
        help_text="Password (min 8 characters, not common)"
    )
    password_confirm = serializers.CharField(
        write_only=True,
        required=True,
        style={'input_type': 'password'},
        help_text="Password confirmation (must match password)"
    )
    
    class Meta:
        model = User
        fields = [
            'id',
            'username',
            'email',
            'password',
            'password_confirm',
            'first_name',
            'last_name',
        ]
        extra_kwargs = {
            'first_name': {'required': False},
            'last_name': {'required': False},
            'email': {'required': True},
        }
    
    def validate_email(self, value: str) -> str:
        """
        Ensure email is unique (case-insensitive).
        
        Args:
            value: Email address to validate
            
        Returns:
            Lowercased email if valid
            
        Raises:
            ValidationError: If email already exists
        """
        email_lower = value.lower()
        if User.objects.filter(email=email_lower).exists():
            raise serializers.ValidationError(
                "A user with this email already exists."
            )
        return email_lower
    
    def validate_username(self, value: str) -> str:
        """
        Ensure username is unique (case-insensitive).
        
        Args:
            value: Username to validate
            
        Returns:
            Lowercased username if valid
            
        Raises:
            ValidationError: If username already exists
        """
        username_lower = value.lower()
        if User.objects.filter(username=username_lower).exists():
            raise serializers.ValidationError(
                "A user with this username already exists."
            )
        return username_lower
    
    def validate(self, attrs: dict) -> dict:
        """
        Validate that password and password_confirm match.
        
        Args:
            attrs: Dictionary of field values
            
        Returns:
            Validated attributes
            
        Raises:
            ValidationError: If passwords don't match
        """
        if attrs['password'] != attrs['password_confirm']:
            raise serializers.ValidationError({
                "password_confirm": "Passwords do not match."
            })
        return attrs
    
    def create(self, validated_data: dict) -> User:
        """
        Create a new user with hashed password and ECDH keys.
        
        This method:
        1. Removes password_confirm (not stored)
        2. Creates user with hashed password (Django handles hashing)
        3. Generates ECDH key pair via crypto module
        4. Stores public key in UserProfile
        5. Stores private key in PrivateKey model
        
        Args:
            validated_data: Validated registration data
            
        Returns:
            Created User instance
        """
        # Remove password_confirm - not needed for user creation
        validated_data.pop('password_confirm')
        
        # Extract password for hashing
        password = validated_data.pop('password')
        
        # Create user instance (password NOT set yet)
        user = User(**validated_data)
        
        # Hash and set password using Django's secure hashing
        # This uses PBKDF2 by default - password is NEVER stored plain text
        user.set_password(password)
        user.save()
        
        logger.info(f"Created new user: {user.username}")
        
        # Generate ECDH key pair for the new user
        self._generate_user_keys(user)
        
        return user
    
    def _generate_user_keys(self, user: User) -> None:
        """
        Generate ECDH key pair for the user.
        
        Uses the crypto module to generate real cryptographic keys.
        
        Args:
            user: User instance to generate keys for
        """
        try:
            # Import here to avoid circular imports
            from crypto.utils import generate_ecdh_key_pair, serialize_public_key, serialize_private_key
            from users.models import PrivateKey
            
            # Generate ECDH key pair using P-256 curve
            private_key, public_key = generate_ecdh_key_pair()
            
            # Serialize keys to PEM format
            public_key_pem = serialize_public_key(public_key)
            private_key_pem = serialize_private_key(private_key)
            
            # Store public key in UserProfile (safe to share)
            user.profile.set_public_key(public_key_pem)
            
            # Store private key securely (NEVER exposed via API)
            PrivateKey.objects.create(
                user=user,
                encrypted_private_key=private_key_pem
            )
            
            logger.info(f"Generated ECDH key pair for user: {user.username}")
            
        except Exception as e:
            logger.error(f"Failed to generate keys for user {user.username}: {str(e)}")
            # Don't fail registration if key generation fails
            # Keys can be generated later


class CustomTokenObtainPairSerializer(TokenObtainPairSerializer):
    """
    Custom JWT token serializer that includes user data in response.
    
    Extends the default token serializer to:
    1. Include user information in the login response
    2. Mark user as online upon successful login
    3. Update last activity timestamp
    
    Response Example:
    {
        "status": "success",
        "message": "Login successful",
        "access": "eyJ0eXAi...",
        "refresh": "eyJ0eXAi...",
        "user": {
            "id": 1,
            "username": "john_doe",
            "email": "john@example.com",
            "first_name": "John",
            "last_name": "Doe",
            "is_online": true
        }
    }
    """
    
    def validate(self, attrs: dict) -> dict:
        """
        Validate credentials and generate tokens.
        
        Args:
            attrs: Dictionary with username and password
            
        Returns:
            Token data with user information
        """
        # Call parent validation (handles authentication)
        data = super().validate(attrs)
        
        # Mark user as online
        self.user.set_online(True)
        
        # Add user information to response
        data['user'] = {
            'id': self.user.id,
            'username': self.user.username,
            'email': self.user.email,
            'first_name': self.user.first_name,
            'last_name': self.user.last_name,
            'is_online': self.user.is_online,
            'has_public_key': hasattr(self.user, 'profile') and self.user.profile.has_valid_key(),
        }
        
        logger.info(f"User logged in: {self.user.username}")
        
        return data


class UserProfileSerializer(serializers.ModelSerializer):
    """
    Serializer for viewing and updating user profile.
    
    Allows updates to:
    - first_name, last_name
    
    Read-only fields:
    - id, username, email, is_online, last_activity, created_at, public_key
    
    Note: public_key is read-only as it's generated automatically.
    """
    public_key = serializers.SerializerMethodField()
    has_public_key = serializers.SerializerMethodField()
    
    class Meta:
        model = User
        fields = [
            'id',
            'username',
            'email',
            'first_name',
            'last_name',
            'is_online',
            'last_activity',
            'created_at',
            'public_key',
            'has_public_key',
        ]
        read_only_fields = [
            'id', 'username', 'email', 'created_at',
            'last_activity', 'is_online', 'public_key', 'has_public_key'
        ]
    
    def get_public_key(self, obj) -> str:
        """Get user's public key (truncated for display)."""
        if hasattr(obj, 'profile') and obj.profile and obj.profile.public_key:
            # Return first 100 chars for display
            return obj.profile.public_key[:100] + '...' if len(obj.profile.public_key) > 100 else obj.profile.public_key
        return None
    
    def get_has_public_key(self, obj) -> bool:
        """Check if user has a valid public key."""
        if hasattr(obj, 'profile') and obj.profile:
            return obj.profile.has_valid_key()
        return False


class PasswordChangeSerializer(serializers.Serializer):
    """
    Serializer for changing user password.
    
    Validates:
    1. Old password is correct
    2. New password meets requirements
    3. New password confirmation matches
    
    Request Example:
    {
        "old_password": "OldPass123!",
        "new_password": "NewSecurePass456!",
        "new_password_confirm": "NewSecurePass456!"
    }
    
    Response Example:
    {
        "status": "success",
        "message": "Password changed successfully"
    }
    """
    old_password = serializers.CharField(
        required=True,
        style={'input_type': 'password'},
        help_text="Current password"
    )
    new_password = serializers.CharField(
        required=True,
        validators=[validate_password],
        style={'input_type': 'password'},
        help_text="New password (min 8 characters)"
    )
    new_password_confirm = serializers.CharField(
        required=True,
        style={'input_type': 'password'},
        help_text="Confirm new password"
    )
    
    def validate(self, attrs: dict) -> dict:
        """
        Validate that new passwords match.
        
        Args:
            attrs: Dictionary of field values
            
        Returns:
            Validated attributes
            
        Raises:
            ValidationError: If new passwords don't match
        """
        if attrs['new_password'] != attrs['new_password_confirm']:
            raise serializers.ValidationError({
                "new_password_confirm": "New passwords do not match."
            })
        return attrs


class PublicKeySerializer(serializers.Serializer):
    """
    Serializer for retrieving a user's public key.
    
    Used during key exchange to get another user's public key
    for deriving the shared secret.
    
    Response Example:
    {
        "status": "success",
        "user_id": 2,
        "username": "alice",
        "public_key": "-----BEGIN PUBLIC KEY-----\n..."
    }
    """
    user_id = serializers.IntegerField(read_only=True)
    username = serializers.CharField(read_only=True)
    public_key = serializers.CharField(read_only=True)

### 5. Updated Views

````python
// filepath: backend/users/views.py
"""
Authentication views for user management.

This module provides REST API endpoints for:
1. User registration (with automatic ECDH key generation)
2. User login (JWT token generation)
3. User logout (token blacklisting)
4. Profile management
5. Password change

All endpoints return consistent JSON responses in the format:
{
    "status": "success" | "error",
    "message": "Description",
    "data": { ... }  // Optional data payload
}
"""
from rest_framework import generics, status, permissions
from rest_framework.response import Response
from rest_framework.views import APIView
from rest_framework_simplejwt.views import TokenObtainPairView, TokenRefreshView
from rest_framework_simplejwt.tokens import RefreshToken
from rest_framework_simplejwt.exceptions import TokenError
from django.contrib.auth import get_user_model
import logging

from .serializers import (
    UserSerializer,
    UserRegistrationSerializer,
    CustomTokenObtainPairSerializer,
    UserProfileSerializer,
    PasswordChangeSerializer,
)

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
User = get_user_model()


class RegisterView(generics.CreateAPIView):
    """
    User Registration API.
    
    POST /api/v1/auth/register/
    
    Creates a new user account with:
    1. Validated and hashed password (Django PBKDF2)
    2. Automatically generated ECDH key pair
    3. Public key stored in UserProfile
    4. Private key stored securely (never exposed)
    
    Request Body:
    {
        "username": "john_doe",
        "email": "john@example.com",
        "password": "SecurePass123!",
        "password_confirm": "SecurePass123!",
        "first_name": "John",  // Optional
        "last_name": "Doe"     // Optional
    }
    
    Response (201 Created):
    {
        "status": "success",
        "message": "User registered successfully",
        "user": {
            "id": 1,
            "username": "john_doe",
            "email": "john@example.com",
            "first_name": "John",
            "last_name": "Doe"
        }
    }
    
    Error Response (400 Bad Request):
    {
        "status": "error",
        "message": "Validation failed",
        "errors": {
            "email": ["A user with this email already exists."]
        }
    }
    """
    queryset = User.objects.all()
    permission_classes = [permissions.AllowAny]  # Registration is public
    serializer_class = UserRegistrationSerializer
    
    def create(self, request, *args, **kwargs):
        """Handle user registration."""
        serializer = self.get_serializer(data=request.data)
        
        if serializer.is_valid():
            user = serializer.save()
            
            logger.info(f"New user registered: {user.username}")
            
            return Response({
                'status': 'success',
                'message': 'User registered successfully',
                'user': {
                    'id': user.id,
                    'username': user.username,
                    'email': user.email,
                    'first_name': user.first_name,
                    'last_name': user.last_name,
                }
            }, status=status.HTTP_201_CREATED)
        
        return Response({
            'status': 'error',
            'message': 'Registration failed',
            'errors': serializer.errors
        }, status=status.HTTP_400_BAD_REQUEST)


class LoginView(TokenObtainPairView):
    """
    User Login API - Returns JWT tokens.
    
    POST /api/v1/auth/login/
    
    Authenticates user and returns:
    1. Access token (30 min expiry)
    2. Refresh token (7 day expiry)
    3. User information
    
    Request Body:
    {
        "username": "john_doe",
        "password": "SecurePass123!"
    }
    
    Response (200 OK):
    {
        "status": "success",
        "message": "Login successful",
        "access": "eyJ0eXAiOiJKV1QiLCJhbGciOiJIUzI1NiJ9...",
        "refresh": "eyJ0eXAiOiJKV1QiLCJhbGciOiJIUzI1NiJ9...",
        "user": {
            "id": 1,
            "username": "john_doe",
            "email": "john@example.com",
            "first_name": "John",
            "last_name": "Doe",
            "is_online": true,
            "has_public_key": true
        }
    }
    
    Error Response (401 Unauthorized):
    {
        "status": "error",
        "message": "No active account found with the given credentials"
    }
    """
    serializer_class = CustomTokenObtainPairSerializer
    
    def post(self, request, *args, **kwargs):
        """Handle login request."""
        serializer = self.get_serializer(data=request.data)
        
        try:
            serializer.is_valid(raise_exception=True)
        except Exception as e:
            logger.warning(f"Login failed for user: {request.data.get('username', 'unknown')}")
            return Response({
                'status': 'error',
                'message': 'Invalid username or password'
            }, status=status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED)
        
        data = serializer.validated_data
        
        return Response({
            'status': 'success',
            'message': 'Login successful',
            'access': data['access'],
            'refresh': data['refresh'],
            'user': data['user']
        }, status=status.HTTP_200_OK)


class LogoutView(APIView):
    """
    User Logout API.
    
    POST /api/v1/auth/logout/
    
    Logs out the user by:
    1. Blacklisting the refresh token
    2. Marking user as offline
    
    Requires: Authentication
    
    Request Body:
    {
        "refresh": "eyJ0eXAiOiJKV1QiLCJhbGciOiJIUzI1NiJ9..."
    }
    
    Response (200 OK):
    {
        "status": "success",
        "message": "Successfully logged out"
    }
    """
    permission_classes = [permissions.IsAuthenticated]
    
    def post(self, request):
        """Handle logout request."""
        try:
            # Get refresh token from request
            refresh_token = request.data.get('refresh')
            
            if refresh_token:
                # Blacklist the refresh token
                token = RefreshToken(refresh_token)
                token.blacklist()
            
            # Mark user as offline
            request.user.set_online(False)
            
            logger.info(f"User logged out: {request.user.username}")
            
            return Response({
                'status': 'success',
                'message': 'Successfully logged out'
            }, status=status.HTTP_200_OK)
            
        except TokenError as e:
            logger.warning(f"Logout with invalid token for user: {request.user.username}")
            # Still mark user as offline even if token is invalid
            request.user.set_online(False)
            
            return Response({
                'status': 'success',
                'message': 'Successfully logged out'
            }, status=status.HTTP_200_OK)
        
        except Exception as e:
            logger.error(f"Logout error: {str(e)}")
            return Response({
                'status': 'error',
                'message': 'Logout failed'
            }, status=status.HTTP_400_BAD_REQUEST)


class RefreshTokenView(TokenRefreshView):
    """
    Token Refresh API.
    
    POST /api/v1/auth/token/refresh/
    
    Gets a new access token using the refresh token.
    
    Request Body:
    {
        "refresh": "eyJ0eXAiOiJKV1QiLCJhbGciOiJIUzI1NiJ9..."
    }
    
    Response (200 OK):
    {
        "status": "success",
        "access": "eyJ0eXAiOiJKV1QiLCJhbGciOiJIUzI1NiJ9..."
    }
    """
    
    def post(self, request, *args, **kwargs):
        """Handle token refresh."""
        try:
            response = super().post(request, *args, **kwargs)
            return Response({
                'status': 'success',
                'access': response.data.get('access'),
                'refresh': response.data.get('refresh')  # New refresh token if rotation enabled
            }, status=status.HTTP_200_OK)
        except Exception as e:
            return Response({
                'status': 'error',
                'message': 'Invalid or expired refresh token'
            }, status=status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED)


class UserProfileView(generics.RetrieveUpdateAPIView):
    """
    User Profile API - Get/Update current user profile.
    
    GET /api/v1/auth/profile/
    Returns the authenticated user's profile.
    
    PUT /api/v1/auth/profile/
    Updates the authenticated user's profile.
    
    Requires: Authentication
    
    GET Response (200 OK):
    {
        "status": "success",
        "user": {
            "id": 1,
            "username": "john_doe",
            "email": "john@example.com",
            "first_name": "John",
            "last_name": "Doe",
            "is_online": true,
            "last_activity": "2024-02-06T10:30:00Z",
            "created_at": "2024-01-15T08:00:00Z",
            "has_public_key": true
        }
    }
    
    PUT Request Body:
    {
        "first_name": "Jonathan",
        "last_name": "Doe"
    }
    """
    permission_classes = [permissions.IsAuthenticated]
    serializer_class = UserProfileSerializer
    
    def get_object(self):
        """Return the authenticated user."""
        # Update last activity on profile access
        self.request.user.update_activity()
        return self.request.user
    
    def retrieve(self, request, *args, **kwargs):
        """Get user profile."""
        instance = self.get_object()
        serializer = self.get_serializer(instance)
        
        return Response({
            'status': 'success',
            'user': serializer.data
        }, status=status.HTTP_200_OK)
    
    def update(self, request, *args, **kwargs):
        """Update user profile."""
        partial = kwargs.pop('partial', False)
        instance = self.get_object()
        serializer = self.get_serializer(instance, data=request.data, partial=partial)
        
        if serializer.is_valid():
            serializer.save()
            
            return Response({
                'status': 'success',
                'message': 'Profile updated successfully',
                'user': serializer.data
            }, status=status.HTTP_200_OK)
        
        return Response({
            'status': 'error',
            'message': 'Profile update failed',
            'errors': serializer.errors
        }, status=status.HTTP_400_BAD_REQUEST)


class PasswordChangeView(APIView):
    """
    Password Change API.
    
    POST /api/v1/auth/password/change/
    
    Changes the authenticated user's password.
    
    Requires: Authentication
    
    Request Body:
    {
        "old_password": "OldPass123!",
        "new_password": "NewSecurePass456!",
        "new_password_confirm": "NewSecurePass456!"
    }
    
    Response (200 OK):
    {
        "status": "success",
        "message": "Password changed successfully"
    }
    
    Error Response (400 Bad Request):
    {
        "status": "error",
        "message": "Current password is incorrect"
    }
    """
    permission_classes = [permissions.IsAuthenticated]
    
    def post(self, request):
        """Handle password change."""
        serializer = PasswordChangeSerializer(data=request.data)
        
        if not serializer.is_valid():
            return Response({
                'status': 'error',
                'message': 'Validation failed',
                'errors': serializer.errors
            }, status=status.HTTP_400_BAD_REQUEST)
        
        user = request.user
        
        # Verify old password using Django's secure comparison
        if not user.check_password(serializer.validated_data['old_password']):
            return Response({
                'status': 'error',
                'message': 'Current password is incorrect'
            }, status=status.HTTP_400_BAD_REQUEST)
        
        # Set new password (Django handles secure hashing)
        user.set_password(serializer.validated_data['new_password'])
        user.save()
        
        logger.info(f"Password changed for user: {user.username}")
        
        return Response({
            'status': 'success',
            'message': 'Password changed successfully'
        }, status=status.HTTP_200_OK)


class CurrentUserView(APIView):
    """
    Get Current Authenticated User API.
    
    GET /api/v1/auth/me/
    
    Returns the currently authenticated user's information.
    Simple endpoint for checking authentication status.
    
    Requires: Authentication
    
    Response (200 OK):
    {
        "status": "success",
        "user": {
            "id": 1,
            "username": "john_doe",
            "email": "john@example.com",
            "first_name": "John",
            "last_name": "Doe",
            "is_online": true,
            "has_public_key": true
        }
    }
    """
    permission_classes = [permissions.IsAuthenticated]
    
    def get(self, request):
        """Get current user info."""
        user = request.user
        user.update_activity()
        
        return Response({
            'status': 'success',
            'user': {
                'id': user.id,
                'username': user.username,
                'email': user.email,
                'first_name': user.first_name,
                'last_name': user.last_name,
                'is_online': user.is_online,
                'has_public_key': hasattr(user, 'profile') and user.profile.has_valid_key(),
            }
        }, status=status.HTTP_200_OK)
